The time required to process your refund will depend on the payment method and whether further information is required.
Withdrawing and tuition fee refunds
If you are a current student and wish to withdraw from your course, you must complete the withdrawal process first before requesting a refund. Submitting a refund request form will not be considered as a formal request to withdraw.
To start your withdrawal, please contact your Student Support Adviser , who will guide you through the process.
Once your withdrawal is completed, your tuition fees will be recalculated based on how long you studied. A refund can only be issued if the credit on your account is greater than any non-refundable deposit you are liable to pay.
Need help
If you have any questions about the refund process, please contact Hallam Help . If you are an international applicant, you can also use direct messaging in your CAS Shield account.
Please note that individual requests for updates cannot be responded to. Updates will be provided automatically by email at each stage of the process.

How to keep your money safe
Things to consider before making a payment
Only paying from your account or that of a close relative or friend you can trust
Do not pay your tuition fee to anyone other than the University
Do not share your personal details to allow others to pay on your behalf
Be aware of anyone other than the University offering to pay your fees at a discount as this could be fraudulent
All applicants/students are advised to pay directly themselves or by international bank transfer using Flywire, information can be found on Payment methods | Sheffield Hallam University
Below are some important tips to help you
Only ever use the University recommended payment method to pay your tuition fees
Do not allow anyone other than a close family member or friend to pay tuition fees on your behalf
Never share your bank account or debit/credit card details with anyone unless you know and can trust them
Be wary of offers of easy money. If it sounds too good to be true, then it probably is
Don’t allow someone that you do not know or trust to transfer money through your bank account
The University will NEVER refund someone who didn’t make the original payment
All refunds will be returned via the same method in which it was received

===== p127-c01 | Hallam Hardship Fund | 300 words
https://www.shu.ac.uk/funding/financial-difficulties/hallam-hardship-fund

Financial support
Contact Hallam Help if you:
• are experiencing financial difficulties, • have a complex funding issue, or • would like help to improve your money skills. We will offer guidance on the support and funding available.
Hallam Hardship Fund
Support for students experiencing unforeseen financial difficulties.
The information on this page is correct for the 2026/27 academic year.
Overview
The Hallam Hardship Fund offers one-off payments to support students experiencing unforeseen financial difficulties. It is intended to help cover essential living costs and ease short-term financial pressure that may affect your ability to continue your studies.
The support we offer may not always be financial, and we may invite you to an appointment to discuss alternative funding and support.
Financial difficulties can affect your mental health and wellbeing. If you're struggling, our Student Wellbeing service provides support and resources that can help.
Our Money and Mental Health Mini Module also offers practical guidance and takes around 15 minutes to complete.
What you could receive
Normally, awards will not exceed £600 .
How are applications assessed
There is no automatic entitlement to an award and applications are assessed on their individual merits.
When assessing your application, we will consider the following factors that may support your eligibility for financial support:
You had a reasonable plan in place to fund your studies before enrolling.
Your financial difficulties were unforeseeable.
There is a risk of harm without financial support.
Your financial situation is having a significant impact on your ability to study.
We will also consider the following factors that may limit your eligibility for financial support:
You have access to other significant sources of funding or savings.
Your bank statements show a high level of non-essential spending.
Your spending habits have not changed following the onset of your financial difficulties.

===== p127-c02 | Hallam Hardship Fund | 292 words
https://www.shu.ac.uk/funding/financial-difficulties/hallam-hardship-fund

We will also consider the following factors that may limit your eligibility for financial support:
You have access to other significant sources of funding or savings.
Your bank statements show a high level of non-essential spending.
Your spending habits have not changed following the onset of your financial difficulties.
You have prioritised non-essential costs (socialising for example) over essential costs (rent or priority bills for example).
Your financial situation appears unsustainable even with additional support.
To help us understand your income and expenditure, we may ask you to securely share your financial information through Open Banking. In most cases, this means you will not need to upload bank statements to your application.
Who can apply
You can apply if you:
are studying a taught credit-bearing course or research degree at Sheffield Hallam University during the 2026/27 academic year,
have ‘home’ fee status,
are studying a course that attracts a fee payable directly to Sheffield Hallam University,
are willing to securely share your financial information through Open Banking, and
have a UK bank account in your name.
If you receive benefits and/or any other financial support, it is your responsibility to check whether receiving an award will affect your entitlement(s)/payments.
If you are an international student, you can apply for the International Emergency Fund .
When to apply
Applications are open throughout the academic year. However, if all available funds have been allocated, applications may close before the end of the academic year.
How to apply
You will need to submit an application form. The form will ask you for:
Information about your personal circumstances.
Information about your unforeseen financial difficulties.
Funding and benefit entitlement letters.
Bank statements.
Relevant supporting evidence.
You must read the terms and conditions before applying.
What happens next

===== p127-c03 | Hallam Hardship Fund | 167 words

How to apply

Bank statements.

If financial information is not shared and other information is not provided when requested and/or if your case is complex, a decision can take up to six weeks.

===== p128-c01 | Emergency vouchers | 380 words
https://www.shu.ac.uk/funding/financial-difficulties/emergency-vouchers

Financial support
Contact Hallam Help if you:
• are experiencing financial difficulties, • have a complex funding issue, or • would like help to improve your money skills. We will offer guidance on the support and funding available.
Emergency vouchers
Emergency vouchers are available to students in immediate financial crisis.
Overview
Emergency vouchers provide temporary assistance if you have an immediate financial crisis. You can request a voucher if you are unable to buy food, toiletries or other essential items and have no other alternatives.
What you could receive
If your request is successful, we will send you a £45 digital Tesco voucher.
There is no guarantee that a voucher will be issued if you request one, and all requests are considered on their individual merits. There is no cash alternative, and we are unable to offer vouchers for other supermarkets.
Vouchers are not a long-term solution to financial difficulties and repeated requests will be refused.
We want to support students who are experiencing emergency financial situations. If you request a voucher, you are likely to be referred to have an appointment with the Student Funding Team and/or your Student Support Adviser.
Who can apply
You can request a voucher if you have home fee status and enrolled on a taught credit bearing course, or research degree, during the current academic year.
We can only issue vouchers to international students following a referral. If you are an international student experiencing financial difficulties you must speak to an adviser from the International Experience Team .
How to apply
You will need to submit a request form. The form will ask you for:
Evidence to support your request (such as bank statements)
A personal statement telling us what has led to your current situation
You must read the guidance on providing bank statements before submitting a request.
What happens next
We will review your request and aim to contact you on the same day, or the next working day at the latest.
If your request is successful, we will email you instructions on how to activate and use your voucher.
If your request is not successful, we will explain why and offer alternative support where possible.
Need help
If you have any questions about requesting a voucher, please contact Hallam Help .

===== p129-c01 | Exceptional Support Fund | 375 words

Financial support

Exceptional Support Fund
The NHS Exceptional Support Fund is available for students on some health courses.
Overview
The Exceptional Support Fund (ESF) is part of the NHS Learning Support Fund and is available to students studying certain health courses. It is for students experiencing unforeseen financial hardship, who have exhausted all other means of funding.
This fund is administered and funded by the NHS Business Services Authority (NHSBSA), not Sheffield Hallam University.
What you could receive
Successful applicants will receive a grant from the NHSBSA.
Who can apply
You can apply for the Exceptional Support Fund if you:
are eligible for the NHS Learning Support Fund,
have applied for the Hallam Hardship Fund (regardless of the outcome), and
have applied for all other viable sources of funding.
You can still apply even if your Hallam Hardship Fund application was unsuccessful.
If you receive benefits and/or any other financial support, it is your responsibility to check whether receiving an award will affect your entitlement(s)/payments.
How to apply
Applications for the Exceptional Support Fund are assessed by the NHSBSA.
To apply, follow these steps:
Log in to your NHS Learning Support Fund account
Download and fill in the Exceptional Support Fund form
Email a copy of your completed form to studentfunding@shu.ac.uk (do not include your supporting evidence)
The Student Funding team will countersign your form, send it to the NHSBSA on your behalf and notify you by email
Once you have received confirmation that we have sent your countersigned form to the NHSBSA , you must send your supporting evidence to nhsesf@nhsbsa.nhs.uk
For advice on your application and the supporting evidence you need to provide, read the guidance from the NHSBSA .
What happens next
The NHSBSA will contact you with the outcome of your application. They will aim to do this within 15 working days of receiving your application.
Need help
If you have any questions about this process, please contact Hallam Help .
For support with the application form and supporting evidence, please contact the NHSBSA .

===== p130-c01 | Budgeting | 270 words
https://www.shu.ac.uk/funding/money-skills/budgeting

Financial support
Contact Hallam Help if you:
• are experiencing financial difficulties, • have a complex funding issue, or • would like help to improve your money skills. We will offer guidance on the support and funding available.
Budgeting
The first step to good budgeting is working out your income.
Then list everything you regularly spend money on. Do you have enough income to cover your expenses? Think about what is essential and what you can cut down on.
We also recommend you think about setting some money aside each month for savings.
Help with budgeting
You can access budgeting tools and courses provided by Blackbullion , a money management service that is free for students – just sign in with your Hallam email address. Here are three of their tools to get you started:
The budget decision tree - helps you balance your income and expenses
Budget calculator tool - understand where your money is being spent
Budgeting 101 pathway - find out what budgeting style suits you
Money Wellness also has a Student Budget Calculator , it is free and no sign-up is needed.
Track your spending
It's important to have sight of your spending so that you can track where your money is going.
Budget tracker apps can help you with this. We recommend:
Moneybox - set up an investment or savings account from your mobile phone.
Emma - a budget tracker app that links to your online banking
Appointments
If you would like the Student Funding team to help you with budgeting and managing your expenses, you can request an appointment by contacting Hallam Help .

===== p131-c01 | Fraud and scams | 288 words
https://www.shu.ac.uk/funding/money-skills/fraud-and-scams

Financial support
Contact Hallam Help if you:
• are experiencing financial difficulties, • have a complex funding issue, or • would like help to improve your money skills. We will offer guidance on the support and funding available.
Fraud and scams
Stay vigilant and scam-savvy!
Every year, students in the UK are scammed by criminals trying to steal money, and/or financial and personal details.
Criminals often use publicly available information to impersonate trusted organisations like the Student Loans Company to try and get confidential information from you. This information is then used to sign into your accounts, steal information and money.
Anyone can be affected by a scam at any time. If it happens to you, it’s not your fault. Report it and seek help as soon as you can.
Here’s some useful information to help to spot scams and protect yourself.
Reporting a scam
If you think you’ve been a victim of or witnessed a scam, report it to the police through Report Fraud as soon as you can.
You can report activity you suspect as money muling by contacting your local police, the HMRC Fraud Hotline , or Crime Stoppers .
If criminals have already got your personal information report it to Report Fraud . If you have been a victim of a cyber crime use the Government's Report a Cyber Incident page to find out how and where it should be reported.
What scams do criminals use?
Phishing is normally done via email. It aims to trick you into giving out your personal details or asks you to click on unknown links
Smishing is done via text message to request data or ask you to click on unknown links.
Vishing is done over the phone.

===== p131-c02 | Fraud and scams | 383 words
https://www.shu.ac.uk/funding/money-skills/fraud-and-scams

Phishing is normally done via email. It aims to trick you into giving out your personal details or asks you to click on unknown links
Smishing is done via text message to request data or ask you to click on unknown links.
Vishing is done over the phone. Criminals pretend to be a person or business and try to get you to provide your personal details.
Targeting students to become money mules . This is where you let someone else use your bank account to send money into.
How do I spot scams and protect myself?
Often emails are sent in bulk. Be wary of emails without any personalisation or ones that are addressed to ‘Dear student’.
Check the quality of the communication - look for poor spelling and grammar.
If you’ve been contacted by an organisation, check the email address is valid by searching online first.
Look out for communications that include a sense of urgency by asking you to reply in a set timeframe, for example if you do not act immediately your account may be closed.
Be cautious of unexpected communications around the time you’re due your student loan payment.
Don’t share personal information or news of receiving your student loan online.
Avoid logging into your bank or student finance account on public networks or computers.
Don’t feel pressured into giving any information on unexpected phone calls. You can hang up at any time. You can find the correct number and call the organisation back yourself to check if it was genuine.
Be suspicious of job adverts that offer the chance to earn quick and easy money. Always stick to reputable job sites.
Don’t share bank and personal details with anyone you don’t know or trust – even among friends or family.
Resources
Scams: how you can avoid them - GOV.UK (www.gov.uk)
Information about money mules via the national crime agency
Scam awareness on Blackbullion
These tools and courses are provided by Blackbullion. You can use them for free - just sign in with your Hallam email address.
Scams pathway - learn about and recognise scams online
Risk pathway - explore practical tips on making wise financial decisions
5 types of fraud - get up to speed on fraud
Phone scam cheat sheet - how to identify a fraudulent call

Financial support
Contact Hallam Help if you:
• are experiencing financial difficulties, • have a complex funding issue, or • would like help to improve your money skills. We will offer guidance on the support and funding available.
Student Success Scholarship
Financial support to help you succeed at Sheffield Hallam.
The information on this page is correct for the 2026/27 academic year.
Overview
The Student Success Scholarship is designed to help you succeed at Sheffield Hallam.
What you could receive
Successful applicants will receive an award of between £600 and £2,100. Most students will receive an award from the lower end of this scale. Only students who are in several priority groups will be considered for the highest amount.
P art-time students will receive a proportional award.
You can only receive the scholarship once per academic year, and you need to reapply each academic year.
Who can apply
You can apply for the Student Success Scholarship if you:
are fully enrolled,
are studying an undergraduate, PGCE or integrated masters course,
have 'home' fee status,
are paying a 2026/27 tuition fee of £9,790, or the foundation year fee of £5,760, or the part-time equivalent (this can be self-funded or through a student loan),
have been means tested by a UK student funding body (such as Student Finance England),
have a household income of £62,410 or less, and
have consented to share your household income with the University as part of the means testing process.
Although you need to be means tested, you do not have to take out a maintenance loan to be eligible, more information can be found on the troubleshooting page. If you cannot be means tested, please let us know by contacting Hallam Help .
You are not eligible for this scholarship if:

===== p133-c02 | Student Success Scholarship | 297 words
https://www.shu.ac.uk/funding/scholarships-and-bursaries/student-success-scholarship

If you cannot be means tested, please let us know by contacting Hallam Help .
You are not eligible for this scholarship if:
you receive a teacher education scholarship/bursary,
you are studying a School-Centred Initial Teacher Training (SCITT) course,
you receive an NHS Training Grant or Social Work Bursary, or
it would negatively affect your eligibility for another scholarship/bursary.
If you receive benefits and/or any other financial support, it is your responsibility to check whether receiving an award will affect your entitlement(s)/payments.
When to apply
There are two application windows planned for the 2026/27 academic year, but you can only receive the scholarship once per academic year.
Application windows and expected payment dates:
10am on Monday 7 September 2026 to 5pm on Sunday 1 November 2026 – payments expected to be made at the start of December
10am on Wednesday 6 January 2027 to 5pm on Sunday 28 February 2027 – payments expected to be made at the start of April
Subject to available funds, application windows may close earlier than the published date.
If you do not provide your bank account details when asked to do so, the expected payment dates will not apply, your payment will be delayed and may be cancelled.
How are applications assessed
To determine if you are eligible for an award and how much your award will be, we will look at your household income* and the priority groups you are in.
Our priority groups are:
Care leavers
Students who are estranged from their family
Students with caring responsibilities
Students who have refugee status
Transgender students, inclusive of non-binary genders
Student parents
Students with a disability*
Final year students*
Student from minority ethnic backgrounds*
Students studying a high-cost or high-intensity course*
*You will not be asked about these in your application.

Enrol online
When you have received your email inviting you to enrol, you should read through the important information below. Then, when you are ready to enrol, click on the 'Enrol on your course now' button further down this page.
Before you enrol
You need an active Sheffield Hallam IT account to enrol. If you don't have one yet, activate your account .
If you’re an international taught student, you need to pay the required amount of your fees before you enrol (if you are unsure of the amount, please refer to your offer letter). Go to the international fee payment page. This does not apply to international research students.
When should I enrol?
We typically send enrolment invitations one to two weeks before your course start date. The exact date will vary by course.
When you receive the email, enrol as soon as you can. You must enrol by your course start date.
You need to enrol to get loan payments, and there is often a gap before your first payment arrives. Try to enrol early, so that money reaches your account before the start of term.
What you need
During enrolment you will need:
Your IT account username and password
Scanned copies of your entry qualifications
A form of photo ID
There is a reminder of your username in the email we sent to you inviting you to enrol. If you have forgotten your password, you can reset it from the enrolment login screen or via the Password Self Service .
If you don't know your username and can't find your enrolment email, check your spam or junk email folders. The subject line of the email is 'Time to enrol at Sheffield Hallam'.
International students

International students

A prepayment of £8000, less your deposit, or 100% of the tuition fee if this is less than £8000, is required to enrol.
If you are a student with a non-British/non-Irish nationality you will remain provisionally enrolled after you complete the online enrolment task. To complete your enrolment you must book and attend an appointment to verify your original documentation with UKVI requirements.
Payment deadlines
Online payments take up to ten days, and bank transfers up to four weeks. To ensure you can enrol on time, pay by the following dates:
Bank transfers: Pay by one month before your course starts
Online payments: Pay by 10 days before your course starts
For more information, read our guidance on payment methods for international students .

Your passport
Your visa or other immigration documents
There is a full list of documentation you might be asked for in our enrolment guidance for international students .

Why does my course name still include ‘with Foundation Year’ when I’ve already completed it?
‘with Foundation Year’ will show against your course name for the duration of your studies, as this is the full course title. When you have completed your foundation year (Level 0), your course name will not change. You will continue to enrol onto this course name each year until you graduate.

Enrolment guidance for international students
All students at Sheffield Hallam must enrol before they start their course. Enrolment is a form of contract with the University; enrolling means that you accept the terms and conditions of this contract. Once you have enrolled fully, you have full access to all University facilities and resources.
Enrolment is online, so you can access it before arriving in the UK. However, to become fully enrolled there are further tasks you must complete after you have arrived in the UK. You must allow time for this before the start date of your course.
The following step-by-step guidance will help you with the enrolment process:
You will have received an unconditional offer of a place at the University. You must accept this offer before you can enrol.
Activate your student IT account .
If you have an international fee status and are a postgraduate taught or undergraduate applicant starting a new course in September 2026, you were required to pay your deposit before you received your Confirmation of Acceptance for Study (CAS) from Sheffield Hallam.
You will then need to pay a total of £8,000, less your deposit, or 100% of the tuition fee if this is less than £8000, before you can enrol at the University. Find out how you can pay here . You cannot fully enrol until the payment has cleared.
It is recommended that payment of tuition fees is received by the University no later than 4 September 2026 to give enough time for the payment to be cleared. Payments received after this date may delay your enrolment.
Enrolment will begin at the start of September 2026 for September-start courses. You will receive an email inviting you to enrol at Sheffield Hallam. Save the email as it contains important information.

===== p139-c02 | Enrolment guidance for international students | 271 words
https://www.shu.ac.uk/welcome/enrol/international

Payments received after this date may delay your enrolment.
Enrolment will begin at the start of September 2026 for September-start courses. You will receive an email inviting you to enrol at Sheffield Hallam. Save the email as it contains important information.
If you have an issue with your enrolment task it may be because the University has not yet received your fee payment. If you have allowed enough time for the payment to arrive (see point 3), please follow the instructions on your enrolment task to upload evidence of payment.
You must enrol before your course starts – the recommended enrolment date is 14 September 2026 and the deadline to enrol is 28 September 2026. You can complete the enrolment task before you arrive in the UK, but you will remain provisionally enrolled until you arrive in the UK and complete the final part of your enrolment (see point 9). This is normal and nothing to worry about; once you have activated your IT account you will have access to IT systems like Blackboard and your timetable.
All students who have declared a non-British or non-Irish nationality must attend an on-campus document checking appointment as part of the enrolment process. Refer to the International Document Checking page for more information about the document checking event and the documents you will need to bring with you.
You will receive an email confirming you are fully enrolled once you have completed the above steps and had your documents verified. If we require further information or documents, we will send you an email, so please check your Sheffield Hallam email account regularly.

===== p141-c01 | Your SHUcard | 279 words

Your SHUcard
How to get a SHUcard
All cards are requested via My Student Record on the ‘My Enrolment and Fees‘ page . SHUcards are produced on-site; for students with a Sheffield postcode, SHUcards should be collected from City Campus, at either the Owen Building Level 5 (S1 2LX) or Head Post Office (S1 2BA) reception points. Students who live outside of Sheffield can request for their SHUcard to be posted or collected at point of order. Posted cards are sent via second class mail to the address provided.
New Students
All students must be fully enrolled to get a SHUcard. New students will be prompted to apply for their SHUcard after they have fully enrolled.
Applications with incomplete addresses will be rejected.
A £10 lost card fee will be charged for a replacement card when the wrong address has been provided.
Students will need to upload a .jpg, .jpeg, or .png photo when making their application.
Any photos that do not meet the guidelines will be rejected.
Posted cards can take up to 10 days to arrive in the UK.
Access to the library is automatic. Any other access linked to studies can be added if required by the subject. This is carried out at either the Owen Building Level 5 (S1 2LX) or Head Post Office (S1 2BA) reception points .
For help and support with enrolment, please see our Welcome pages, or access our Hallam Helper virtual assistant on MyHallam .
Existing students/staff
To order new or replacement staff cards. All cards need to be ordered on an individual basis.
SHUcard application - Staff form
To order all other student cards:
SHUcard application - Student form

To order all other student cards:
SHUcard application - Student form
To access the form, students must be logged into Microsoft services using their Sheffield Hallam email address in the following format: studentID@hallam.shu.ac.uk .
Lost or damaged cards
A £10 charge must be paid for lost or damaged cards. This is paid for via the online store SHU Online store before the SHUcard Application - Student order can be completed.
Customers are required to register with the SHU Online Store before payment can be made. This is not single sign on.
New photos can be uploaded if required.
If your card is stolen, a crime reference number is required to get a replacement card free of charge.
Returning students/expired cards
Returning students who have out-of-date cards will need to enter the old card details when completing SHUcard Application - Student .
Students must be logged into Microsoft services using their Sheffield Hallam email address in the format of: studentID@hallam.shu.ac.uk to access the form.
Once a new card is produced all access, including the library, will cease to work on the old card.
SHUcards can be requested to be collected in person or to be posted.
Library access is automatic; any other access must be linked to the subject. This needs to be requested at the time of application and will take 48 hours to activate.
Please include your Student/Staff ID in any correspondence.
If you have any problems ordering via the online store, please email onlinestore@shu.ac.uk .
If you need help obtaining a SHUcard please phone 0114 225 5555 Monday to Friday 8:30am - 4:30pm or email SHU-student-card-replacement@shu.ac.uk .

===== p142-c01 | International arrivals | 296 words
https://www.shu.ac.uk/welcome/prepare/international-arrivals

International arrivals
Welcome to Sheffield Hallam!
We’re excited to welcome you to Sheffield and look forward to meeting you soon.
Arriving for September 2026
To be ready for Orientation and your course induction, please plan to arrive in Sheffield by Thursday 10 September 2026 at the latest.
Orientation takes place on Friday 11 September 2026 and covers essential information you need to succeed, from settling in to understanding key university services. Attendance is crucial, so please arrive on time to make sure you don’t miss important guidance or opportunities to meet other students.
If you’re unable to attend on 11 September 2026, please email us at studentimmigrationcompliance@shu.ac.uk to request a later arrival date.
If you arrive outside of our Meet and Greet desk opening hours and you need assistance, you can call our 24-hour helpline: 0800 073 1318 – make sure to save this number before you travel.
Don't forget to contact your accommodation/landlord in advance to confirm your arrival time.
Timeline
Follow our timeline and complete the key tasks to ensure a smooth transition to study in Sheffield Hallam.
Key tasks
Pay your fees
If you are an international postgraduate taught or undergraduate applicant you are required to pay a deposit, which will be quoted on your offer letter, before you receive your Confirmation of Acceptance for Study (CAS) from Sheffield Hallam.
A prepayment of £8000, less your deposit, or 100% of the tuition fee if this is less than £8000, is required on re-enrolment each year.
Find out more about paying your fees , including payment methods and important payment dates .
Get support
Before you arrive in the UK you can join our online Pre-departure Briefings , live Q&A sessions, or watch video presentations. G et answers to your preparation and arrival questions.

Once you are in Sheffield, visit our on-campus International Meet & Greet desk with your questions, as many times as you need.
Location: Entrance (level 5), Owen building, City Campus , Howard Street, Sheffield, S1 1WB
10am – 4pm, Monday to Friday. From 7 – 25 September 2026.
Plan your arrival
Activating your eVisa
If you are studying in the UK on a Student visa, you must activate your eVisa before your immigration documents can be checked and you can complete enrolment.
Getting your immigration documents checked
All students who have declared a nationality which is not British or Irish must attend an on-campus document checking event as part of the enrolment process.

===== p144-c01 | Preparing for study | 290 words
https://www.shu.ac.uk/welcome/prepare/preparing-for-study

Preparing for study
Courses and information to get you ready for University-level study.
Key tasks
Check your University emails
Your online learning hub
Get software and equipment
Email is an important and widely used method of communication at university and in the workplace. You can find out how to access and use your university email on Hallam Digital Skills Email guide .
We recommend that you check your email regularly before you arrive, as we will be sending you reminders about pre-arrival tasks and Welcome Week activities.
It also includes equipment suggestions, and tips on getting the best out of your online sessions.
Your library
Whatever your course looks like, you'll need the right tools and strategies to help you study effectively. Your library is here to help you build the skills you need to succeed - here's how we can support you:
24/7/365 access to silent, quiet and group learning spaces, PCs and laptops across both campus libraries (Adsetts and Collegiate)
Subject guides with key books, resources, and databases to explore your subject in more depth
Interactive group sessions and how-to guides on critical reading, writing, referencing, and assessment preparation
Digital tools and training to help you stay organised, manage references, and use AI responsibly
Find out more on the Library Welcome page .
Blackboard - Your online learning hub
Blackboard is our teaching hub. You'll need it for a lot of things, including:
checking your timetable,
finding course materials,
accessing email and files,
getting grades and feedback, and
communicating with tutors and other students.
Our Blackboard guide tells you more, and you can access Blackboard through MyHallam as soon as you have enrolled (please note it can take 2/3 days for your timetable to be available following enrolment).

accessing email and files,

As a Hallam student you can get free access to a range of software and learning resources:
Microsoft 365
LinkedIn Learning
Specialist software to support your studies
You will also have specific software needed for your course. Please ask your course leader before buying any software. You will need to be enrolled to access this software.
Hallam Digital Skills Support
The Hallam Digital Skills website is your self-help guide to getting support with technology at university. It has online resources to help you use tools like Microsoft 365, Blackboard, study skills and more. You can explore it anytime to improve your skills at your own pace.
What's on offer:
Step-by-step guides on tools like Microsoft 365, Turnitin, and data analysis software
Free access to LinkedIn Learning platform
Library digital workshops to find out about the digital skills support on offer

View your timetable and find class times and locations
Get important updates and course messages
View activities, coursework, exams and grades via Blackboard
Log class attendance on Study Goal
Find your way around with our Campus Maps
Book rooms, equipment and PCs
Get study and wellbeing support 24/7
Access your email and other must-have uni apps
